Potential algorithm: Alternating updates to maximize entropy? There is a known iterative method for minimax convolution: For given p, define r_i = active indicator or weights based on q_i. Update p_i ← p_i / (1 + λ ∑_{k active} p_{k-i})? Let's derive subgradient mirror descent.

Take log p variables u_i, p_i=e^{u_i}/Z. We want minimize max q. Subgradient of smoothed max: s_i = q_i / (∑e^K q?) or active. Gradient wrt log p of q? If p_i scaled multiplicatively, note q is homogeneous degree2: ∑ p_j ∂q/∂p_j =2q. A multiplicative update p_i ← p_i - η g_i may not preserve sum. Mirror descent natural gradient: update direction in log p: δu_i = - η (∂φ/∂p_i). This tends to reduce proportional. If use all active set, ∂φ/∂p_i=2ρ_i, where ρ_i=∑_{k active} p_{k-i} as above. This raises/lower p relative to contributions to bad sums.

We can do: 
- Normalize p sum=1.
- Compute q=conv(p,p), scale maybe.
- Active set A = top K coefficients or those > threshold (e.g., 0.9 qmax). Let h_i=∑_{k∈A} p_{k-i} (length n). This is 2D convolution of active indicator and p.
- Gradient g_i = 2h_i. Subgradient descent on p: p ← p - η g. Project to positive and sum=1. But subtracting from p can make many zero. Use multiplicative: p ← p / (1 + η h_i) (since g positive). Then normalize. The factor denominator positive; reduces coordinates with high gradient. Equivalent mirror descent for sum constraint? Let's check: For log p, gradient p*g; multiplicative exp(-η g) ~1/(1+ηg). Then normalize. This maintains full support. This is a standard heavy-ball? It should lower active q.

Need choose η. Since p sum=1, gradient scale ~? h_i sum_i h_i = (sum p)*(#active) ≈ K_active. Coordinates order 1/n. For active set ~n, h_i order? Could be O(√n/n)? Need adapt. But each line search many convolutions. Can compute q efficiently after update.

Another powerful idea: Because objective is max of quadratics, we can use column generation / balancing via dynamic programming? We can formulate as find p such that all q_k ≤ M. For a candidate M, this is a feasibility of p≥0, ∑p=1, q_k≤M. Could solve via value iteration? Constraints are quadratic but if we introduce variables x_i = p_i? Not convex. Objective and gradient implementation details:
```
OPT_M = ...
OPT_K = ...
def objective_u(u):
    p = softmax(u)
    r, P = conv_r(p, m)
    # maybe cap r for exp
    shift = r.max()
    ex = np.exp(OPT_K*(r-shift))
    # clamp ex
    Z = ex.sum()
    w = ex/Z
    grad_p = 2*m * cross_corr(w, p)
    grad_u = p*(grad_p - np.dot(p, grad_p))
    # exact value maybe 2*max(r) or smooth
    return 2*(shift + np.log(Z)/OPT_K - np.log(2*m-1)/OPT_K), grad_u
```

Direct mirror descent:
```
def mirror_improve(p, m, iters, K?):
    p = normalize(p)
    for it in range(iters):
       r = n*conv
       # active weights: use softmax with K=200 maybe
       w = softmax(K*(r-rmax))
       h = cross_corr(w,p)
       g = h # gradient positive
       # choose step size eta: line search over [0.001,1]
       best_eta = ...
       p = p / (1+eta*g)
       p = clip, normalize
       if score improved break/adjust
```

1. Helpers:
   - `safe_mean(x)` etc.
   - `fft_convolution_r(p, m)`: returns r = m*conv coefficients real, maybe clipped negative to 0.
       Use nfft = 1 << (2*m-2).bit_length()
       P = np.fft.rfft(p, n=nfft)
       C = np.fft.irfft(P*P, n=nfft)[:2*m-1].real
       C[C<0]=0 (due to roundoff)
       return m*C
   - `cross_corr_weights(w, p, m, nfft?)`:
       W = np.fft.rfft(w, n=nfft?) But w length 2m-1; p length m. Need nfft ≥ (2m-1)+m-1 = 3m-2 for circular convolution if using P*conj(W) formula? Let's examine. We want h_i = ∑_j p_j w_{i+j}, i=0..m-1, j=0..m-1. This is cross-correlation of p and w. The length of h is m; maximum index i+j up to 2m-2. Formula using P(ω) conj(W(ω)) where P length m, W length 2m-1. For DFT cyclic length N, product's coefficient h_i includes wraparound terms from indices outside 0..2m-2? We must choose N ≥ (m-1)+(2m-2)=3m-3 to avoid circular aliasing if using P*conj(W) coefficient with sign? Let's derive: P(ω)=∑_{j=0}^{m-1} p_j e^{-ijω}. conj(W)=∑_{k=0}^{2m-2} w_k e^{ikω}. Product ∑_{j,k} p_j w_k e^{i(k-j)ω}. If N finite, coefficient i mod N includes pairs where k-j ≡ i mod N. Max k-j =2m-2, min=-(m-1). Range length 3m-3. To avoid alias, N≥3m-3. That is larger (for m=6121, 18360, next pow2 32768). Still okay. Alternatively compute h by direct convolution with reversed w using FFT length N≥2m-1? Let's find a convolution formula with smaller N.
       h_i = ∑_{j=0}^{m-1} p_j w_{i+j}. Define w_rev_t = w_{2m-2-t}. Then h_i = ∑_j p_j w_{i+j}. What convolution gives? (p * w_rev)_? conv[t]=∑_j p_j w_rev_{t-j} = ∑ p_j w_{2m-2-(t-j)} = ∑ p_j w_{2m-2-t+j}. Set 2m-2-t+j = i+j => t=2m-2-i. So h_i = (p * w_rev)_{2m-2 - i}. Great! p length m, w_rev length 2m-1, convolution length 3m-2? Wait length m + (2m-1)-1 =3m-2, but index 2m-2-i up to 2m-2 (within first 2m-1?) Minimum i=m-1 => index m-1.        - wr = w[::-1]

   Need support multiple starts without redefining objective each time. We'll use global variables `_M`, `_K`, maybe `_REG`.
   ```
   def _smooth_jac(x):
       m = _M; K = _K
       p = softmax(x)
       r = fft_conv_r(p,m)
       shift = r.max()
       # handle if not finite
       ex = np.exp(K*(r-shift))
       denom = ex.sum()
       w = ex/denom
       h = cross_corr_weights(w,p,m)
       gp = 2.0*m*h
       gp = gp - np.dot(p,gp)  # vector? subtract scalar from all
       gu = p*gp
       # add tiny reg
       gu += 2e-10*x
       val = 2*(shift + np.log(denom)/K - np.log(2*m-1)/K) + 1e-10*np.dot(x,x)
       if not finite: return inf, zeros
       return val, gu
   ```
   Note `gp` gradient of objective wrt p includes 2m. Then subtract mean_wrapping. The p*gp already has zero sum. Good.

   ```
   def mirror_refine(p, m, iters, time_limit):
       p = normalize; best_p=p; best=score_r
       for it in range(iters):
           if time... break
           r=fft_conv_r(p,m)
           # active weights: use softmax K=200? But r max O1, differences maybe 0.01; K=100.
           shift=r.max(); ex=np.exp(200*(r-shift)); w=ex/ex.sum()
           h=cross_corr_weights(w,p,m)
           base=best
           # scale: g median positive
           g=h
           scale=np.median(g[g>1e-300]) if any else 1.0
           for eta in [0.2,0.5,1,2,4,8,16] / max(scale,1e-12): maybe too many F
               np_ = p/(1+eta*g)
               np_ = np.clip(np_,0,None); sum; if sum<=0 continue; np_/=sum
               sc=score_r(np_,m)
               if sc<base: ... break
           if no improvement: eta *=0.5 for several, then break/restart
           p=np_best
       return p
   ```

def _softmax(x):
    y = x - np.max(x)
    # clip for safe
    y = np.clip(y, -50, 50) # underflow not issue but to avoid overflow? x could large; subtract max leaves <=0. max zero, min maybe -1000; clip -50 may distort. Use -700.
    e = np.exp(y)
    s = e.sum()
    if s==0 or not finite: return uniform
    return e/s
```
Clipping min to -50 may flatten very small coordinates; but p min can be 1e-20 in long searches. For numerical stability, use -745? exp(-745) underflows; okay. Use `y = np.clip(y, -700, 0)`.
But if x has large positive mean, subtract max makes max0. Fine.

`_fft_convolve_r(p,m)`:
```
N = 1 << (2*m-2).bit_length()
P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
C = np.fft.irfft(P*P, N)[:2*m-1].real
C = np.maximum(C,0)
return m*C, P? maybe not
```
Repeated allocation. For same m, N fixed; could use global? In helper no state; okay. To improve, in `_corr` we also need r. We can combine to compute P once for objective? But each objective call needs r and h. For gradient, P for r and W for corr. Could compute C via `P = rfft(p,N); C=irfft(P*P,N)`. Then for h, need W = rfft(wr,Ncorr). We can do separately. Fine.

`_cross_corr_weights(w,p,m)`:
```
N = 1 << (3*m-2).bit_length()
wr = w[::-1]
P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
W = np.fft.rfft(wr, N)
conv = np.fft.irfft(P*W, N)
# take indices m-1 to 2m-2 reversed
h = conv[2*m-2:m-2:-1]  # start 2m-2 down to m-1 inclusive length m
# Actually slice syntax stop exclusive: h = conv[2*m-2 : m-2 : -1]
return np.maximum(h,0)
```
Check slice for m=2: conv[2:0:-1] -> indices 2,1 yes. For m=1: 2*m-2=0, m-2=-1, conv[0:-1:-1]? empty? Need handle m=1 separately: p length1, w length1, h0=p0. if m==1 return p.copy().
For m>1, conv[2*m-2 : m-2 : -1] includes 2m-2 down to m-1. Good.

`_resample_p(p_old, m_old, m_new)`:
```
c = np.cumsum(p_old); total=c[-1]; c=c/total
target=(np.arange(m_new)+0.5)/m_new
# Ensure c unique? If repeated due zeros, np.interp with repeating c works? It maps to first index. okay.
positions=np.interp(target, c, np.arange(m_old), right=m_old-1, left=0)
left=np.floor(positions).astype(int); right=np.minimum(left+1,m_old-1); frac=positions-left
p_new=p_old[left]*(1-frac)+p_old[right]*frac
return normalize
```
This quantile resampling may distort density at endpoints but okay. Maybe direct density interpolation better: `x_old=np.linspace(0,1,m_old); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,m_new); p_new=np.interp(x_new,x_old,p_old); normalize`. Which is better? For probability masses, CDF resampling preserves quantile and shape; direct density may have endpoint interpolation. Use CDF.

Initial generator:
```
def _make_p(m, kind, a=0.0, b=0.0, rng=None):
    x=np.arange(m)
    if kind==0: p=np.ones(m)
    elif kind==1: p=(x+0.5)**(-a)
    elif kind==2: p=(m-x-0.5)**(-a)
    elif kind==3: p=(x+0.5)**(-a)*(m-x-0.5)**(-b)
    elif kind==4: p=1.0/(np.sqrt(x+0.5)+a)
    elif kind==5: p=np.exp(-a*x/m)
    elif kind==6: p=np.exp(-a*(m-x-0.5)/m)
    elif kind==7: p=np.exp(-a*(x-0.5*m/bit?)^2) ...
    elif kind==8: p=np.exp(rng.normal(0,b,x)) maybe.
    elif kind==9: p = mixture f0 + exponential: p = A/(sqrt(x+0.5)) + B exp(-a x/m)
```
For kind3, if a+b close to 1? Fine. Add floor 1e-15, normalize.

Seeds for m:
- We want deterministic but randomized with rng. Use multiple kinds:
   left power alpha: [0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6,0.3,0.7?] all; but if alpha>1? p0 finite, support shorter; maybe.
   right power same.
   denominator c: [0,0.01,0.05,0.1,0.3,1.0]
   weighted sum: p=0.5*f0_left +0.5*exp(-a x/m) for a [0.5,1,2,4]
   random smooth: p=exp(a*x/m + b*(x/m)^2 + noise), normalize.
Maybe 10 seeds per small m.

Use same.
        R = _fft_R(p,m)
        shift=R.max()
        ex=np.exp(80*(R-shift))
        w=ex/ex.sum()
        h=_cross_corr(w,p,m)
        # median positive
        med = np.median(h[h>1e-300]) if np.any(h>1e-300) else 1.0
        if med<=0: med=1.0
        base_eta = 1.0/med
        tried=False
        for eta in [base_eta*f for f in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1,2,5,10]]:
            q = p/(1.0+eta*h)
            q = np.clip(q, 0.0, None)
            s=q.sum()
            if s<=0: continue
            q/=s
            r_new=_score_r(q,m)
            if r_new < best_r - 1e-12:
                best_r=r_new; best_p=q.copy(); p=q; stagnant=0; tried=True; break
        if not tried:
            # try random perturb + small
            q = p * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,size=m))
            q = _normalize(q)
            r_new=_score_r(q,m)
            if r_new < best_r:
                p=q; best_p=q; best_r=r_new
            else:
                stagnant +=1
                if stagnant>10: break
    return best_p, best_r
```

def _next_pow2(n): return 1 << (int(n)-1).bit_length()? if n=1 ->1.

def _fft_R(p, m):
    if m==1: return np.array([1.0]) if p[0]? Actually R = m*C, sum? For p sum1, C=[1], R=[1].
    n = 2*m-1
    N = 1 << (n-1).bit_length()
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(P*P, N)[:n].real
    np.maximum(C, 0.0, out=C)
    return m*C
```
`_score_r(p,m)`: `return float(np.max(_fft_R(p,m)))`

`_cross_corr(w,p,m)`:
```
if m==1: return p.copy()
nconv = 3*m-2
N=1<<(nconv-1).bit_length()
wr=w[::-1]
P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
W=np.fft.rfft(wr,N)
conv=np.fft.irfft(P*W,N)
h=conv[2*m-2 : m-2 : -1].copy()
np.maximum(h,0,out=h)
return h
```

   p2? val huge

Implement top selection:
```
def _top_candidates(p_list, m, k):
   scored = [(_score_r(p,m), p) for p in p_list]
   finite = [s for s in scored if np.isfinite(s[0])]
   finite.sort(key=lambda t: t[0])
   selected=[]
   for _,p in finite[:k]:
       if p not in selected: selected.append(p) # can't compare arrays; just unique by object identity not needed.
   return [p for r,p in finite[:k]]
```

Let's include shapes: mixture f0 + uniform segments. For discrete, endpoint singular f0 p=(i+1)^-0.5 normalized; box segment length L. We'll generate:
```
def _mixture_f0_box(m, lam, a, b, rng=None):
    base=(np.arange(m)+0.5)**-0.5; base/=base.sum()
    mask=(np.arange(m) >= a*m) & (np.arange(m) < b*m)
    if box count zero: continue
    box=np.zeros(m); box[mask]=1.0; box/=box.sum()
    p=lam*base + (1-lam)*box
    return normalize
```
But lam base mass: base already normalized; p sum lam+(1-lam)=1. Good.

def _shifted_singular(m, a,b): arr zeros; idx = arange(a*m,b*m); arr[idx]=(idx-a*m+0.5)^-0.5; normalize.

```
def _try_zero_threshold(p, m, thresholds=[1e-8,1e-10,1e-12]):
   best=p; best_r=_score
   for th in thresholds:
      q=p.copy(); q[q<th* p.max()] =0; normalize; r=score; if r<best_r ...
   return best
```

Can't nested `add`. We can write helper top-level `_valid_norm_append`? Or just in loops after each p: `cands.append(_safe_p(p))`. Top-level `_safe_p(p)` returns normalized or None.
```
def _safe_p(p):
    p=np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim!=1 or p.size==0 or not np.isfinite(p).any(): return None
    p=np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    s=p.sum()
    if s <= 0: return None
    p = p/s
    # add floor? no
    return p
```
Then in candidates use `pp=_safe_p(...); if pp is not None: cands.append(pp)`.

`_resample_p`: If m_old==m_new, return normalize. If p old sum1.
```
def _resample_p(p, m_old, m_new):
    if m_old == m_new: return _safe_p(p)
    p=_safe_p(p).astype(float)
    c=np.cumsum(p)
    s=c[-1]
    if s<=0: return np.ones(m_new)/m_new
    c = c/s
    # if c has zero at start? p[0] maybe 0; c[0]=0. np.interp with duplicate x? It works but target less than c[1] maps to 0.
    target=(np.arange(m_new)+0.5)/m_new
    pos=np.interp(target, c, np.arange(m_old, dtype=float), left=0.0, right=m_old-1.0)
    left=np.floor(pos).astype(np.int64)
    right=np.minimum(left+1, m_old-1)
    frac=pos-left
    out=p[left]*(1-frac)+p[right]*frac
    return _safe_p(out)
```
If p has many zeros, c duplicates; np.interp expects xp increasing, duplicates okay? It may return first corresponding y for target equal to duplicate; for target in a plateau (zero mass), pos jumps; that's okay, probability measure. But if duplicate values in c with different y (zero mass gaps?), `np.interp` with non-strictly increasing xp is allowed? It may behave undefined? To be safe, remove duplicates: build arrays of unique c with average? Simpler: use direct density interpolation if p has zeros. Let's do direct density interpolation; less risk.
```
if m_old == m_new: normalize
if m_new < m_old? direct: x_old=np.linspace(0,1,m_old), x_new=np.linspace(0,1,m_new); out=np.interp(x_new, x_old, p); normalize.
```
Direct density preserves endpoint features and sum approx. It doesn't strictly preserve probability quantile but good. But if p_old has zero tail, direct interpolation okay. Use direct.
```
x_old = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m_old)
x_new = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m_new)
out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
return _safe_p(out)
```

Now, `_safe_p` should not floor; but if p has inf/nan. Add max value? If p huge due exp, normalize may overflow? In shapes, p could be extremely large if alpha 0.8? `(x+0.5)**-0.8` p0 finite ~1; okay. For random logp clipped. If p has inf, `np.isfinite` fails. Use nan_to_num? We'll handle.
```
def _safe_p(p):
    p=np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size == 0: return None
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.where(p>0, p, 0.0)
    s=p.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0: return None
    p = p/s
    # if any >1? normalize ensures <=1
    return p
```
If posinf set to0 may drop; okay.

`_run_minimize`: Need if `maxiter<=0 or not HAVE_SCIPY`: return x0. Setting globals may persist. Use try.
```
def _run_minimize(m, x0, obj_type, K, maxiter):
    global _G_M, _G_K, _G_OBJ
    x0=np.asarray(x0,float).copy()
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or maxiter<=0 or m==0: return x0
    _G_M=int(m); _G_K=float(K); _G_OBJ=int(obj_type)
    jac = _smooth_jac if obj_type==0 else _l2_jac
    options = {'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'ftol': 1e-9, 'gtol': 1e-5, 'maxcor': 15, 'maxfun': int(max(1000, maxiter*30))}
    try:
         res = minimize(jac, x0, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, options=options)
         if hasattr(res,'x') and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
             return res.x
    except Exception:
         pass
    return x0
```

Now, `_mirror_refine` step size line search. To reduce FFT calls, we can compute scores only for a few eta. Use `K=100` or 150 for active weights.
```
def _mirror_refine(p,m,deadline,rng):
    p=_safe_p(p); if p is None: return p, inf
    best=p.copy(); best_r=_score_r(p,m)
    eta_factor=1.0
    no_improve=0
    max_iters=500
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       R=_fft_R(p,m)
       shift=R.max()
       ex=np.exp(120.0*(R-shift))
       w=ex/ex.sum()
       h=_cross_corr(w,p,m)
       pos=h[h>1e-300]
       scale = np.median(pos) if pos.size else 1.0
       if scale <=0 or not finite: scale=1.0
       base=1.0/(scale*max(eta_factor,1e-6))
       improved=False
       for f in [0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1,2,5,10]:
          if time ... break
          eta=base*f
          q=p/(1.0+eta*h)
          q=np.where(q>0,q,0); s=q.sum(); if s<=0: continue
          q/=s
          r_new=_score_r(q,m)
          if r_new < best_r - 1e-12:
             best_r=r_new; best=q.copy(); p=q; improved=True; no_improve=0; eta_factor=1.0; break
       if not improved:
          no_improve+=1
          # random perturbation
          noise = 0.03 * max(1.0, 0.5)
          q = p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.05,size=m))
          q = _safe_p(q)
          if q is not None:
             r_new=_score_r(q,m)
             if r_new < best_r:
                p=q; best=q.copy(); best_r=r_new; improved=True; no_improve=0
          if no_improve > 15: break
          # shrink eta_factor? try smaller factors next time
          eta_factor *= 0.5
          if eta_factor < 1e-3:
              # try larger? reset
              eta_factor = 10.0
    return best,best_r
```
The line search's `best_r` only updates if improvement. It may miss a q that improves over current but not best? best is global best, okay. If current p has gotten worse? We always set p to improved q; no worse. Good.
But using `best_r` for comparison means if a step produces a value between current and best? no.
K active 120 maybe 1/N. Good.

def _topk_by_score(cands, m, k):
    scored=[]
    for i, p in enumerate(cands):
        p2=_safe_p(p)
        if p2 is None: continue
        r=_score_r(p2,m)

            scored.append((float(r), i, p2))
    if not scored: return [], []

```python
"""
Progressive softmax L-BFGS/mirror search for low self-convolution sequences.

The optimizer works in the probability simplex.  For a normalized sequence p
(sum 1), the evaluator's value is 2 * n * max(p * p), so the search minimizes
max(p * p) while using n as the density scale.  It uses FFT-based objective
and Jacobian evaluation, L-BFGS with a smooth max approximation, a few L2
stages to spread the sum-distribution, and a final active-set mirror refinement.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

_G_M = 1
_G_K = 80.0
_G_OBJ = 0
_REG = 1e-10
_L2_SCALE = 2.0


def _safe_p(p):
    """Normalize a candidate to the probability simplex; return None if invalid."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size == 0:
        return None
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.where(p > 0.0, p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    p = p / s
    if not np.isfinite(p).all():
        return None
    return p


def _softmax(x):
    """Softmax on real variables."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    y = x - float(np.max(x))
    y = np.clip(y, -740.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(y)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full_like(x, 1.0 / x.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def _fft_R(p, m):
    """Return R_k = m * conv(p,p)_k for k=0..2m-2."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    m = int(m)
    if m == 1:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    n = 2 * m - 1
    N = 1 << max(0, (n - 1).bit_length())
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, n=N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(P * P, n=N)[:n].real
    np.maximum(C, 0.0, out=C)
    return m * C


def _score_r(p, m):
    """Return max_k R_k, i.e. half of the evaluator score for normalized p."""
    R = _fft_R(p, m)
    return float(np.max(R))


def _cross_corr(w, p, m):
    """
    h_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}, for i=0..m-1.
    Used to gradient fields of convolution with weight vector w.
    """
    m = int(m)
    if m == 1:
        return np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    nconv = 3 * m - 2
    N = 1 << max(0, (nconv - 1).bit_length())
    wr = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)[::-1]
    P = np.fft.rfft(np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64), n=N)
    W = np.fft.rfft(wr, n=N)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(P * W, n=N)
    h = conv[2 * m - 2 : m - 2 : -1].copy()
    np.maximum(h, 0.0, out=h)
    return h


def _smooth_jac(x):
    """Smooth maximum objective 2 * max(R) and Jacobian wrt log p variables."""
    m = int(_G_M)
    K = float(_G_K)
    if K <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(K):
        K = 1.0
    p = _softmax(x)
    R = _fft_R(p, m)
    shift = float(np.max(R))
    ex = np.exp(K * (R - shift))
    den = float(ex.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(den) or den <= 0.0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)

    w = ex / den
    h = _cross_corr(w, p, m)
    gp = (4.0 * m) * h
    mu = float(np.dot(p, gp))
    gu = p * (gp - mu)
    gu += (2.0 * _REG) * x

    val = 2.0 * (shift + np.log(den) / K) + _REG * float(np.dot(x, x))
    if not np.isfinite(val) or not np.isfinite(gu).all():
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)
    return float(val), gu


def _l2_jac(x):
    """Smooth L2 stage: minimize mean(R^2), helps broaden the sum distribution."""
    m = int(_G_M)
    p = _softmax(x)
    R = _fft_R(p, m)
    L = 2 * m - 1
    h = _cross_corr(R, p, m)

    val = _L2_SCALE * float(np.dot(R, R)) / L + _REG * float(np.dot(x, x))
    gp = _L2_SCALE * (4.0 * m / L) * h
    mu = float(np.dot(p, gp))
    gu = p * (gp - mu)
    gu += (2.0 * _REG) * x

    if not np.isfinite(val) or not np.isfinite(gu).all():
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)
    return float(val), gu


def _run_minimize(m, x0, obj_type, K, maxiter):
    """Run L-BFGS-B for a short stage.  obj_type: 0 smooth max, 1 L2."""
    global _G_M, _G_K, _G_OBJ
    x0 = np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or maxiter <= 0 or m <= 0:
        return x0

    _G_M = int(m)
    _G_K = float(K)
    _G_OBJ = int(obj_type)
    jac = _smooth_jac if int(obj_type) == 0 else _l2_jac

    options = {
        "maxiter": int(maxiter),
        "ftol": 1e-10,
        "gtol": 1e-5,
        "maxcor": 15,
        "maxfun": int(max(500, maxiter * 25)),
    }
    try:
        res = minimize(jac, x0, method="L-BFGS-B", jac=True, options=options)
        if hasattr(res, "x") and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
            return np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return x0


def _resample_p(p, m_old, m_new):
    """Resample a normalized sequence by linear density interpolation."""
    p = _safe_p(p)
    if p is None:
        return np.ones(int(m_new), dtype=np.float64) / max(1, int(m_new))
    m_old = int(m_old)
    m_new = int(m_new)
    if m_old == m_new:
        return p
    if m_new <= 0:
        return None
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m_old, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m_new, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    return _safe_p(out)


def _left_power(m, alpha):
    return np.power(np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5, -float(alpha))


def _right_power(m, alpha):
    return np.power(m - np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64) - 0.5, -float(alpha))


def _box_base(m, a, b):
    a = max(0.0, min(1.0, float(a)))
    b = max(0.0, min(1.0, float(b)))
    if b <= a:
        return None
    p = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    i0 = int(np.floor(a * m))
    i1 = int(np.ceil(b * m))
    i0 = max(0, min(m, i0))
    i1 = max(0, min(m, i1))
    if i1 <= i0:
        return None
    p[i0:i1] = 1.0
    s = p.sum()
    if s <= 0:
        return None
    return p / s


def _bump_base(m, a, b):
    a = max(0.0, min(1.0, float(a)))
    b = max(0.0, min(1.0, float(b)))
    if b <= a:
        return None
    x = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    t = (x - a * m) / max(1.0, (b - a) * m)
    valid = (t >= 0.0) & (t <= 1.0)
    p[valid] = np.sin(np.pi * t[valid]) ** 2
    s = p.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    return p / s


def _ratio_base(m, c):
    x = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    c = max(0.0, float(c))
    return 1.0 / (np.sqrt(x + 0.5) + c)


def _exp_base(m, a, reverse):
    x = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    if reverse:
        x = m - 1 - x
    a = max(0.0, float(a))
    return np.exp(-a * x / max(1, m - 1))


def _shape_candidates(m, rng, rich):
    """Generate a diverse pool of normalized initial shapes."""
    m = int(m)
    cands = []
    x = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)

    left_alphas = [0.35, 0.42, 0.48, 0.52, 0.58, 0.66]
    if rich <= 1:
        left_alphas = [0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.60]

    for a in left_alphas:
        p = _left_power(m, a)
        q = _safe_p(p)
        if q is not None:
            cands.append(q)

    right_alphas = [0.50, 0.55, 0.65]
    if rich <= 1:
        right_alphas = [0.50, 0.55]

    for a in right_alphas:
        p = _right_power(m, a)
        q = _safe_p(p)
        if q is not None:
            cands.append(q)

    cands_den = [0.0, 0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.60, 1.20]
    if rich <= 1:
        cands_den = [0.02, 0.10, 0.40]
    for c in cands_den:
        p = _ratio_base(m, c)
        q = _safe_p(p)
        if q is not None:
            cands.append(q)

    exp_rates = [0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]
    if rich <= 1:
        exp_rates = [1.0, 2.0]
    for a in exp_rates:
        for rev in (False, True):
            p = _exp_base(m, a, rev)
            q = _safe_p(p)
            if q is not None:
                cands.append(q)

    if rich >= 2:
        two_alphas = [(0.30, 0.05), (0.35, 0.20), (0.45, 0.25), (0.50, 0.30)]
    else:
        two_alphas = [(0.45, 0.15), (0.50, 0.25)]

    for al, br in two_alphas:
        p = np.power(x + 0.5, -float(al)) * np.power(m - x - 0.5, -float(br))
        q = _safe_p(p)
        if q is not None:
            cands.append(q)

    f0 = _safe_p(_left_power(m, 0.5))
    if f0 is not None:
        segs = [
            (0.0, 0.30), (0.0, 0.50), (0.0, 0.70),
            (0.05, 0.45), (0.10, 0.60), (0.15, 0.70),
            (0.20, 0.65), (0.25, 0.75), (0.30, 0.80),
            (0.35, 0.85), (0.40, 0.90), (0.0, 1.0),
        ]
        if rich <= 1:
            segs = [(0.0, 0.50), (0.10, 0.60), (0.25, 0.75), (0.35, 0.85)]

        lams = [0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.95]
        if rich <= 1:
            lams = [0.80, 0.90]

        for lam in lams:
            for a, b in segs:
                b1 = _box_base(m, a, b)
                if b1 is not None:
                    p = float(lam) * f0 + float(1.0 - lam) * b1
                    q = _safe_p(p)
                    if q is not None:
                        cands.append(q)

                b2 = _bump_base(m, a, b)
                if b2 is not None:
                    p = float(lam) * f0 + float(1.0 - lam) * b2
                    q = _safe_p(p)
                    if q is not None:
                        cands.append(q)

    if rich >= 2:
        n_rand = 6
    elif rich == 1:
        n_rand = 3
    else:
        n_rand = 1

    for _ in range(n_rand):
        t = x / max(1, m - 1)
        logp = (
            rng.normal(0.0, 0.30)
            + rng.normal(0.0, 0.70) * t
            + rng.normal(0.0, 0.50) * t * t
            + rng.normal(0.0, 0.06, size=m)
        )
        p = np.exp(np.clip(logp, -20.0, 20.0))
        q = _safe_p(p)
        if q is not None:
            cands.append(q)

    return cands


def _topk_by_score(cands, m, k):
    """Score candidates by R=max and return top k by (score, index)."""
    scored = []
    for i, p in enumerate(cands):
        q = _safe_p(p)
        if q is None:
            continue
        r = _score_r(q, int(m))
        if np.isfinite(r):
            scored.append((float(r), i, q))
    if not scored:
        return [], []
    scored.sort()
    out = []
    vals = []
    for r, idx, q in scored:
        if len(out) >= int(k):
            break
        out.append(q)
        vals.append(r)
    return out, vals


def _random_perturb(p, rng, strength):
    """Random multiplicative/block perturbation used by mirror search."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p
    q = p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(strength), size=n))
    for _ in range(3):
        width = max(1, int(n // 120))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + width)
        q[start:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(strength))))
    return _safe_p(q)


def _mirror_refine(p, m, deadline, rng):
    """
    Active-set multiplicative local search.
    It can explore perturbed basins while always remembering the best sequence.
    """
    m = int(m)
    p = _safe_p(p)
    if p is None:
        return np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / m, np.inf

    best = p.copy()
    best_r = _score_r(p, m)
    current = p.copy()
    current_r = best_r
    no_improve = 0
    eta_factor = 1.0

    max_iters = 900
    for _ in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > float(deadline):
            break

        R = _fft_R(current, m)
        shift = float(np.max(R))
        ex = np.exp(120.0 * (R - shift))
        den = float(ex.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(den) or den <= 0.0:
            break
        w = ex / den
        h = _cross_corr(w, current, m)

        pos = h[h > 1e-300]
        scale = float(np.median(pos)) if pos.size else 1.0
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 0.0:
            scale = 1.0

        base_eta = 1.0 / (scale * max(1e-6, eta_factor))
        improved = False

        factors = [0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0]
        for f in factors:
            if time.monotonic() > float(deadline):
                break
            eta = base_eta * float(f)
            q = current / (1.0 + eta * h)
            q = np.where(q > 0.0, q, 0.0)
            s = float(q.sum())
            if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
                continue
            q = q / s
            r_new = _score_r(q, m)
            if np.isfinite(r_new) and r_new < current_r - 1e-12:
                current = q.copy()
                current_r = r_new
                if r_new < best_r:
                    best_r = r_new
                    best = q.copy()
                improved = True
                eta_factor = 1.0
                break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 6:
                # Jump to a perturbed version of the global best basin.
                noise = 0.12 + 0.05 * min(4.0, float(no_improve) / 4.0)
                jump = _random_perturb(best, rng, noise)
                if jump is not None:
                    r_jump = _score_r(jump, m)
                    if np.isfinite(r_jump):
                        current = jump
                        current_r = r_jump
                else:
                    current = best.copy()
                    current_r = best_r
                no_improve = 0
                eta_factor = 1.0
            else:
                eta_factor *= 0.55
                if eta_factor < 1e-3:
                    eta_factor = 8.0
                    if no_improve > 12:
                        break

    return best, best_r


def _load_global_sequence():
    """Load the provided height_sequence_1 if available and valid."""
    try:
        seq = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821
    except Exception:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.where(arr > 0.0, arr, 0.0)
    if not np.isfinite(arr).all():
        return None
    if arr.sum() <= 0.0:
        return None
    return arr


def _try_zero_threshold(p, m):
    """Try a few aggressive zeroing thresholds; keep the one with lowest R."""
    p = _safe_p(p)
    if p is None:
        return None
    best = p
    best_r = _score_r(p, m)
    mx = float(p.max())
    for th in [1e-8, 1e-7, 1e-6, 1e-5]:
        q = p.copy()
        q[q < th * mx] = 0.0
        q = _safe_p(q)
        if q is None:
            continue
        r = _score_r(q, m)
        if np.isfinite(r) and r < best_r:
            best = q
            best_r = r
    return best


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for a low-self-convolution nonnegative sequence within the time budget.
    """
    seed = int(seed)
    try:
        budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()

    DEFAULT_N = 6121
    global_arr = _load_global_sequence()
    global_exact = None

    if global_arr is not None:
        try:
            gscore = evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in global_arr])  # noqa: F821
            if np.isfinite(gscore):
                global_exact = (global_arr.copy(), float(gscore))
        except Exception:
            global_exact = None

    if global_exact is not None and 1000 <= global_exact[0].size <= 10000:
        final_n = int(global_exact[0].size)
    else:
        final_n = DEFAULT_N

    # Reserve time for final mirror/exact checks.
    opt_deadline = t0 + max(120.0, budget - 240.0)
    final_deadline = t0 + max(0.0, budget - 30.0)

    m_list = []
    if final_n <= 1024:
        m_list.append(max(64, final_n))
        m_list.append(final_n)
    else:
        for v in (256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096):
            if v < final_n:
                m_list.append(v)
        m_list.append(final_n)

    # De-duplicate and enforce increasing.
    tmp = []
    for v in m_list:
        v = int(v)
        if v not in tmp and v > 0:
            tmp.append(v)
    m_list = tmp

    search_best_p = None
    search_best_r = np.inf

    source_list = []  # list of (p, m)
    final_pool = []

    def _add_global_pool(m):
        pool = []
        g = _safe_p(global_arr)
        if g is not None:
            if g.size == m:
                pool.append(g)
            else:
                pool.append(_resample_p(g, g.size, m))
        return pool

    pool = _add_global_pool(m_list[0])
    pool.extend(_shape_candidates(m_list[0], rng, rich=2))
    top_init, top_scores = _topk_by_score(pool, m_list[0], 4)
    if top_init:
        source_list = [(q, m_list[0]) for q in top_init]
        if top_scores[0] < search_best_r:
            search_best_r = top_scores[0]
            search_best_p = top_init[0].copy()

    for mi, m in enumerate(m_list):
        if time.monotonic() > opt_deadline:
            break

        rich = 2 if m <= 1024 else (1 if m <= 2048 else 0)
        is_final = (m == final_n)

        pool = []
        for sp, sm in source_list:
            if sp is None:
                continue
            if sm == m:
                pool.append(sp)
            else:
                pool.append(_resample_p(sp, sm, m))

        if mi == 0 or is_final:
            pool.extend(_add_global_pool(m))
        pool.extend(_shape_candidates(m, rng, rich=rich))

        top_sources, top_source_scores = _topk_by_score(pool, m, 3 if not is_final else 4)
        for p, r in zip(top_sources, top_source_scores):
            if r < search_best_r:
                search_best_r = r
                search_best_p = p.copy()

        optimized_pool = []
        for p in top_sources:
            p = _safe_p(p)
            if p is None:
                continue
            optimized_pool.append(p)
            u = np.log(np.clip(p, 1e-300, None))
            u -= float(np.mean(u))

            if is_final:
                stages = [
                    (1, 1.0, 45),
                    (0, 30.0, 75),
                    (0, 100.0, 75),
                    (0, 300.0, 45),
                ]
            elif m <= 512:
                stages = [
                    (1, 1.0, 80),
                    (0, 30.0, 80),
                    (0, 100.0, 80),
                    (0, 300.0, 50),
                ]
            elif m <= 1024:
                stages = [
                    (1, 1.0, 70),
                    (0, 30.0, 70),
                    (0, 100.0, 70),
                    (0, 300.0, 40),
                ]
            elif m <= 2048:
                stages = [
                    (1, 1.0, 50),
                    (0, 30.0, 55),
                    (0, 100.0, 45),
                    (0, 300.0, 30),
                ]
            else:
                stages = [
                    (1, 1.0, 35),
                    (0, 30.0, 40),
                    (0, 100.0, 35),
                    (0, 300.0, 20),
                ]

            for obj_type, K, iters in stages:
                if time.monotonic() > opt_deadline - 30.0:
                    break
                x = _run_minimize(m, u, obj_type, K, iters)
                p2 = _safe_p(_softmax(x))
                if p2 is None:
                    continue
                r2 = _score_r(p2, m)
                if np.isfinite(r2):
                    optimized_pool.append(p2)
                    if r2 < search_best_r:
                        search_best_r = r2
                        search_best_p = p2.copy()
                u = np.log(np.clip(_safe_p(x), 1e-300, None))
                u -= float(np.mean(u))

        next_sources, next_scores = _topk_by_score(optimized_pool, m, 2 if not is_final else 3)
        source_list = [(q, m) for q in next_sources]
        for p, r in zip(next_sources, next_scores):
            if r < search_best_r:
                search_best_r = r
                search_best_p = p.copy()

        if is_final:
            final_pool.extend(top_sources)
            final_pool.extend(next_sources)

    # Ensure we have at least one candidate at final length.
    if not final_pool:
        if search_best_p is not None:
            p = _resample_p(search_best_p, search_best_p.size, final_n)
            if p is not None:
                final_pool.append(p)
        elif global_arr is not None:
            p = _resample_p(global_arr, global_arr.size, final_n)
            if p is not None:
                final_pool.append(p)
        else:
            final_pool.append(np.ones(final_n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, final_n))

    # Final mirror refinement on a few best FFT candidates.
    final_top, final_scores = _topk_by_score(final_pool, final_n, 3)
    mirror_pool = list(final_top)
    for p in final_top:
        if time.monotonic() > final_deadline:
            break
        q, r = _mirror_refine(p, final_n, final_deadline, rng)
        if q is not None:
            mirror_pool.append(q)
            zq = _try_zero_threshold(q, final_n)
            if zq is not None:
                mirror_pool.append(zq)
            # small stochastic polishing
            for _ in range(2):
                qp = _random_perturb(q, rng, 0.05)
                if qp is not None:
                    rj = _score_r(qp, final_n)
                    if np.isfinite(rj):
                        qpz = _try_zero_threshold(qp, final_n)
                        if qpz is not None:
                            mirror_pool.append(qpz)

    exact_best_seq = None
    exact_best_score = np.inf

    # Use known global exact score if available.
    if global_exact is not None:
        exact_best_seq = global_exact[0].copy()
        exact_best_score = global_exact[1]

    # Exact-evaluate a few strongest FFT candidates. Keep the best exact score.
    eval_top, _ = _topk_by_score(mirror_pool, final_n, 4)
    evaluated_ids = set()
    for p in eval_top:
        if time.monotonic() > final_deadline:
            break
        q = _try_zero_threshold(p, final_n)
        if q is None:
            continue
        key = q.tobytes()
        if key in evaluated_ids:
            continue
        evaluated_ids.add(key)
        try:
            sc = evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in q])  # noqa: F821
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < exact_best_score:
                exact_best_score = float(sc)
                exact_best_seq = q.copy()
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Fallback: use FFT best if exact evaluator failed.
    if exact_best_seq is None:
        if search_best_p is not None:
            exact_best_seq = _resample_p(search_best_p, search_best_p.size, final_n)
        elif final_top:
            exact_best_seq = final_top[0].copy()

    # Prepare a safe normalized sequence for return.
    if exact_best_seq is None:
        seq = np.ones(final_n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        seq = np.asarray(exact_best_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        seq = np.nan_to_num(seq, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        seq = np.where(seq > 0.0, seq, 0.0)
        s = float(seq.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            seq = np.ones(final_n, dtype=np.float64)
            s = float(seq.sum())
        seq = seq / s

    # The evaluator clamps to [0,1000], but keep values well within range.
    seq = np.clip(seq, 0.0, 1.0)
    s = float(seq.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        seq = np.ones(final_n, dtype=np.float64)
        seq = seq / float(seq.sum())

    out = [float(v) for v in seq]
    return out
```
